### [Link](https://powerbi.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/deep-dive-into-composite-semantic-models-with-direct-lake-and-import-tables/)
Here are 4 technical bullet points summarizing the Power BI blog on composite semantic models with Direct Lake and import tables:

* **Mixed storage modes**: Composite semantic models can now combine Direct Lake tables with import tables from various data sources, including lakehouses, warehouses, SQL databases, and mirrored databases, using the Power Query online connectors.
* **Flexibility and conversion**: Import tables can be added to Direct Lake tables, and vice versa, using the "Convert a table in Direct Lake mode to import mode" code example, allowing for flexibility in semantic modeling and data analysis, such as using calculated columns or hierarchies.
* **Improved relationships and performance**: Regular relationships between Direct Lake and import tables maintain report performance, overcoming the limitations of traditional DirectQuery and import composite models, which only supported limited relationships, and enabling more complex data modeling and analysis.
* **Enhanced creation and editing capabilities**: Composite models with Direct Lake and import tables can be created and edited using Power BI web modeling, Power BI Desktop live editing, or a combination of both, with new buttons and features, such as "Get data", "OneLake catalog", "Transform data", and "Refresh", making it easier to manage and refine semantic models.

---
### [Link](https://powerbi.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/modern-visual-tooltips-in-power-bi-generally-available/)
Here are 4 technical bullet points summarizing the Power BI blog on Modern Visual Tooltips:

* The general availability of modern visual tooltips in Power BI enables users to interact with reports more easily, with features such as a built-in actions footer that allows drill down, drill up, and drill through actions directly from the tooltip.
* Modern visual tooltips adopt the report's theme colors and can be customized further using the Format pane, allowing for adjustments to colors, fonts, and transparency to match brand or reporting needs, with the option to import custom themes or customize the current theme.
* The updated tooltips have no impact on existing reports, which will continue to use the old tooltip experience until the report author edits them to use the new styling and actions footer, while new reports will use the modern experience by default.
* Report authors can easily update existing reports to use the new tooltips by selecting "Reset to default" in the Format pane on any visual, which will update all visuals in the report to use the modern experience, including the updated styling and actions footer.

### [Link](https://powerbi.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/deep-dive-into-composite-semantic-models-with-direct-lake-and-import-tables/)
Here are 4 technical bullet points summarizing the Power BI blog on composite semantic models with Direct Lake and import tables:

* Composite semantic models in Power BI now support mixing Direct Lake tables with import tables, allowing for greater flexibility in data modeling and enabling the use of calculated columns and hierarchies in Analyze in Excel.
* The update extends the capabilities of Direct Lake on OneLake table storage mode, which could already mix tables from other Fabric data sources, to now include import tables from any data source using Power Query online, with support for regular relationships and improved performance.
* Power BI web modeling and Power BI Desktop live editing can be used to create and edit composite models with Direct Lake and import tables, with new buttons and options available in the ribbon, including Get data, OneLake catalog, Transform data, and Refresh, to easily add and manage tables.

Here are 4 technical bullet points summarizing the Power BI blog on Modern Visual Tooltips:

* **Enhanced Interactivity**: Modern visual tooltips in Power BI now include an Actions footer, allowing users to perform drill down, drill up, and drill through actions directly from the tooltip, streamlining workflows and making data exploration more intuitive.
* **Theme-Based Styling**: Tooltips adopt the report's theme colors, providing a consistent and professional look across all visuals, and can be further customized using the Format pane or by modifying the report theme, applying changes to all existing and new visuals.
* **Customization and Control**: Report authors can enable or disable the Actions footer and customize tooltip styling, including colors, fonts, and transparency, using the Format pane or by importing a custom theme, with options to update individual visuals or apply changes globally.
* **Backward Compatibility and Adoption**: The update does not impact existing reports, which will retain their original tooltip experience until edited to use the new styling and actions footer, while new reports will use the modern visual tooltips by default, with the option to reset to default and adopt the updated tooltips in existing reports.

### [Link](https://powerbi.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/modern-visual-tooltips-in-power-bi-generally-available/)
Here are 4 technical bullet points summarizing the Power BI blog on modern visual tooltips:

* **Streamlined Interactions**: Modern visual tooltips in Power BI now include an Actions footer, allowing users to perform drill down, drill up, and drill through actions directly from the tooltip, eliminating the need for right-clicking or using the visual header.
* **Theme-Based Styling**: Tooltips automatically adopt the report's theme colors, providing a consistent and professional look across all visuals, and can be further customized using the Format pane or by modifying the report theme.
* **Customization and Control**: Report authors can enable or disable the Actions footer and customize tooltip colors, fonts, and transparency to match their brand or reporting needs, with changes applying to all existing visuals or only individual visuals as desired.
* **Seamless Upgrade**: The update to modern visual tooltips does not impact existing reports, which will retain their original tooltip experience until edited to use the new styling and Actions footer, while new reports will use the modern experience by default, with updated styling and Actions footer enabled.

* The Markers feature in Azure Maps allows for the use of meaningful icons or custom images to represent data points on a map, such as deliveries, facilities, or assets, with the ability to scale marker sizes dynamically based on metrics like shipment volume or sales performance.

### [Link](https://powerbi.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/power-bi-january-2026-feature-summary/)
Here are 4 technical bullet points summarizing the Power BI January 2026 Feature Summary for a professional:

* **Deprecation and Discontinuation**: Power BI Q&A will be deprecated in December 2026, and users are recommended to use Power BI Copilot instead. Additionally, support for SQL Server Reporting Services Report Viewer Web Part will end on April 13, 2026, with no future updates planned.
* **Enhanced Reporting and Visualization**: The January release introduces new reporting tools, smarter data modeling options, and enhanced visualizations, including the introduction of Markers in Azure Maps Visual and the general availability of Modern Visual Tooltips.
* **AI and Copilot Advancements**: Power BI has introduced several AI and Copilot-related features, including the ability to attach a report or semantic model to Copilot chat as a reference point, and the introduction of standalone Copilot access on the Power BI home page.
* **Integration and Accessibility**: The Power BI application is now accessible within Teams on Microsoft 365 Operated by 21Vianet, allowing users to view Power BI content and pin items to chats or channels using Tabs. However, some features such as Power BI notifications and preview cards are not supported in this environment.

Here are 4 technical bullet points summarizing the Power BI Dataviz World Champs for a professional:

### [Link](https://powerbi.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/power-bi-2025-holiday-recap-a-decade-of-innovation-and-impact/)
Here are 4 technical bullet points summarizing the Power BI blog for a professional:

* **Desktop and web data modeling parity**: Power BI has achieved core data modeling parity between Power BI Desktop and Power BI service, allowing users to create and edit semantic models directly in the service, and enabling end-to-end solution development entirely through a web browser.
* **Write Back Within Reports**: The introduction of translytical task flows (Preview) in Power BI enables users to trigger actions and write back data directly within reports, streamlining the process of turning insights into actions.
* **Unified experience with Microsoft Fabric**: Power BI's integration with Microsoft Fabric empowers users with a unified experience for data and AI-driven innovation, allowing creators to leverage the full power of the platform to transform business intelligence at scale.
* **Enhanced data modeling and version history**: The Power BI service now supports semantic model version history, automatically saving changes and allowing users to track and manage different versions of their data models, making it easier to collaborate and manage complex data workflows.

### [Link](https://powerbi.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/power-bi-2025-holiday-recap-a-decade-of-innovation-and-impact/)
Here are 4 technical bullet points summarizing the Power BI blog for a professional:

* **Desktop and web data modeling parity**: Power BI now allows users to create and edit semantic models directly in the Power BI service, establishing core data modeling parity between Power BI Desktop and Power BI service, enabling end-to-end solution development entirely through a web browser.
* **Write Back Within Reports**: The introduction of translytical task flows (Preview) in Power BI enables users to trigger actions and write back data directly within reports, enhancing the platform's ability to support bidirectional data flows and enabling more seamless integration with external systems.
* **Enhanced data ingestion and transformation**: Power BI's data modeling capabilities have been enhanced to support the full workflow, from data ingestion and transformation with Power Query to modeling and report generation, all within the Power BI service, eliminating the need to transition development between the desktop and cloud.
* **Semantic model version history**: Power BI now supports semantic model version history within the service, allowing creators to track changes and maintain a record of all updates made to their models, enhancing collaboration, auditing, and compliance capabilities, and providing a more robust and reliable data modeling experience.

### [Link](https://powerbi.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/power-bi-2025-holiday-recap-a-decade-of-innovation-and-impact/)
Here are four technical bullet points summarizing the Power BI 2025 holiday recap:

* **Desktop and web data modeling parity**: Power BI achieved core data modeling parity between Power BI Desktop and Power BI service, allowing users to create and edit semantic models directly in the Power BI service, and supporting end-to-end solutions entirely through a web browser.
* **Write Back Within Reports**: The introduction of translytical task flows (Preview) in Power BI enables users to trigger actions and write back data directly within reports, streamlining the workflow and making Power BI more actionable.

### [Link](https://powerbi.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/drillthrough-in-excel-now-supported-for-direct-lake-and-directquery-models/)
Here are 4 technical bullet points summarizing the Power BI blog for a professional:

* Power BI has removed the limitation that prevented drillthrough (using Excel's Show Details feature) on Direct Lake and DirectQuery semantic models, now supporting MDX DRILLTHROUGH statements for these models.
* The updated drillthrough feature allows users to double-click a PivotTable cell and retrieve the underlying detail rows, even when connected live to Direct Lake and DirectQuery models, providing a consistent experience across Import, Direct Lake, and DirectQuery models.
* Drillthrough in Direct Lake and DirectQuery models respects all applied filters and security roles, including Row-Level Security (RLS) and Object-Level Security (OLS) configurations, ensuring secure and controlled access to data.
* To use the drillthrough feature, users can connect to their semantic model using Analyze in Excel, ensure they are using explicit measures or fields that support detail rows, and double-click a leaf-level cell in their PivotTable to view the underlying data, with performance depending on the underlying source system and query complexity.

---

* Key feature breakthroughs in 2025 enhanced the platform's functionality, allowing users to leverage data more effectively and drive business decision-making with increased speed and accuracy.
* The 2025 updates and enhancements in Power BI were complemented by community-driven initiatives, including milestone events and global contests, which fostered engagement and collaboration among users, developers, and industry experts.

### [Link](https://powerbi.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/power-bi-2025-holiday-recap-a-decade-of-innovation-and-impact/)
Here are 4 technical bullet points summarizing the Power BI blog for a professional:

* **Data Modeling Parity**: Power BI achieved core data modeling parity between Power BI Desktop and Power BI service, allowing users to create and edit semantic models directly in the Power BI service, and enabling end-to-end solution development entirely through a web browser, including data ingestion, transformation, modeling, and report generation.
* **Translytical Task Flows**: The introduction of translytical task flows (Preview) in Power BI enables users to trigger actions and write back data directly within reports, streamlining the process of turning insights into action and supporting more efficient decision-making workflows.
* **Microsoft Fabric Integration**: Power BI's integration with Microsoft Fabric empowers users with a unified experience for data and AI-driven innovation, enabling creators to leverage the full power of the platform to drive business intelligence and data culture transformation at scale.
* **Power BI Service Enhancements**: The Power BI service now supports automatic saving and version history for semantic models, allowing creators to track changes and collaborate more effectively, and providing a more seamless and intuitive user experience for developing and managing data models and reports.

---
### [Link](https://powerbi.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/drillthrough-in-excel-now-supported-for-direct-lake-and-directquery-models/)
Here are 4 technical bullet points summarizing the Power BI blog for a professional:

* Power BI now supports drillthrough for Direct Lake and DirectQuery semantic models using Excel's Show Details feature, enabling retrieval of underlying detail rows without switching to Import mode.
* The MDX DRILLTHROUGH statement, previously only supported for Import models, is now supported for Direct Lake and DirectQuery models, allowing for a consistent drillthrough experience across all model types.
* Drillthrough in Direct Lake and DirectQuery models respects applied filters and security roles, including Row-Level Security (RLS) and Object-Level Security (OLS) configurations, ensuring secure and accurate data analysis.
* To use drillthrough, Excel users can connect to their semantic model using Analyze in Excel, double-click a leaf-level cell in the PivotTable, and view the underlying data, with performance dependent on the underlying source system and query complexity.

Here are four technical bullet points summarizing the Power BI blog for a professional:

* Power BI now supports drillthrough for Direct Lake and DirectQuery semantic models via Excel's "Show Details" feature, allowing users to retrieve underlying detail rows by double-clicking a PivotTable cell.
* The updated functionality utilizes the MDX DRILLTHROUGH statement, which was previously only supported for Import models, and now enables consistent drillthrough experiences across Import, Direct Lake, and DirectQuery models.
